# Current implementation state

Updated 2026-11-10 during the rejected-codebase repair. This record supersedes the readiness wording in earlier records.

The person rejected commits `a70ed94` and `5e84cfa` as an unacceptable codebase, then explicitly requested code repair and reorganization across the code, technical documents, hooks, checks, E2E tests, guides, skills and knowledge records. They clarified that scratch-pad's engineering practices apply; faster E2E verification is the intended deviation. They subsequently explicitly authorized pushing this incomplete repair for review despite the recorded failures. Current delivery is scoped to Codex and Claude Code; the fake backend supports offline tests and other adapters remain deferred. No readiness acceptance is implied by the review push.

Documentation checks remain independent of vendor end-to-end tests, as the person requested. The reference practices still apply to code, packages, guides, skills and verification.

## Current automatic-startup draft

The root main checkout holds the uncommitted automatic-startup increment. The final source selection passed 245 cases with zero failures across nine files in 15.59 seconds. It includes all six automatic workflow cases, ten daemon startup cases, eight authenticated probe cases, four entry-resolution cases and eight actual run, knowledge, request and CLI MCP connector cases. Its exit 1 is the mandatory omitted whole-product coverage result. Evidence is /tmp/titan-daemon-startup-restored.log. Restored typechecking passed. The complete installed-package probe then exited 0 outside the checkout with external networking disabled. Archive sha1 is ae05c5fcf2acdf02ac3a98968380b5900fc8d98f. Evidence is /tmp/titan-daemon-startup-installed.log. It includes all four automatically started public workflows and invalid-script preservation plus previous installed Codex and Claude stub, recovery, native-tool, request, parallel-plan and watch acceptance.

The first selection passed 132 cases and failed one fixture that expected a numeric exitCode after SIGKILL. The fixture now verifies actual OS exit and the expected kill signal, preserving its cleanup assertion. The repaired selection passed 133 cases before the eight client cases were added. Evidence is /tmp/titan-daemon-startup-first.log, /tmp/titan-daemon-startup-repaired.log and /tmp/titan-daemon-startup-clients.log. The focused ten-case baseline also passed after strengthening the signal assertion.

Five type-correct faults were detected and restored. Ignoring health schema failure caused one assertion failure, dropping authentication caused seven, starting another owner on reuse caused one, omitting forced child cleanup caused one and starting before script validation caused one. Typechecking passed before every fault and after restoration. Evidence is /tmp/titan-daemon-startup-mutants.json. The canonical skill guide now describes automatic startup and foreground serve diagnostics. No live vendor call or Titan skill invocation occurred.

CLI client transports accept optional async connectors while retaining existing explicit caller defaults. Bootstrap attaches the connector to run, request and knowledge clients and starts the daemon before CLI MCP transport. The connector probes authenticated schema-checked health with bounded reads, then launches the source or installed entry with a detached process group and ignored standard streams. It waits through competing-child exit and kills and reaps only its own child on deadline failure. Owner startup_timeout_ms is optional and can reduce the central 5000 ms ceiling. Probe and polling caps are 250 and 25 ms. The CLI declares streams as a workspace dependency. Bun installation refreshed that lock entry and the previously declared fake-backend errors entry without changing dependency versions. The canonical spelling dictionary now includes the actual Linux proc filename cmdline used by exact-home process cleanup tests.

The public example factory registers six automatic startup cases and checks malformed scripts leave no home. Its cleanup verifies a fresh daemon's serve command and exact home in proc metadata before stopping it. The installed probe repeats these automatic examples. Source types, lint, docs, schemas, boundaries, unused checks, spelling and the reference structure check have passed during preparation. Full normal gates remain required.

Five new startup scenarios have installed acceptance and are marked implemented. Overall 22 of 261 tasks are checked, with 510 of 862 scenarios pending. Original task 14.4 remains open for live observer events. Next actions are to run normal commit and push gates, verify exact remote main and disabled Actions, then continue the remaining product contracts. Preserve root .agent, .claude and .codex and all existing stashes.

## Process-owned server lease and catalog

Bounded-reader increment `b469131` was committed and pushed through passing hooks; remote main was verified. Delivery continues toward installed Codex and Claude acceptance. Directory existence could outlive a killed daemon, so ownership now uses a separate persistent SQLite exclusive transaction. The lease database is never unlinked during cleanup. OS process death releases its lock. Only the lease holder may reclaim absent-process legacy metadata. Live, missing and malformed legacy owner records fail closed; process-id reuse can conservatively refuse recovery. Incomplete old-version startup metadata and network filesystems remain unaccepted.

Catalog operations now use Bun SQL promises with bound parameters and Zod result validation. Startup closes both databases on failure. Initial tests caught that the pinned Bun 1.3.10 executed only the first setup statement from a combined SQL string, despite newer documentation. Executing one statement at a time fixed initialization and ensured BEGIN EXCLUSIVE actually runs. The complete offline suite passed 435 tests across 41 files in 33.04 seconds with complete measured line and function coverage and 94 product sources with zero gaps. This timing includes real CLI daemon restarts. An earlier run had all behavior assertions passing but failed aggregate function coverage because an unused backend factory had not executed; reclaimed-owner tests now execute a real fake-backed workflow.

Four CLI process cases verify forced kill, graceful restart, live competition and simultaneous startup. Restart preserves an already completed run byte-for-byte at the response boundary. Seven ownership cases cover reclaimed metadata, fresh discovery, removed live metadata and conservative failure. Replacing BEGIN EXCLUSIVE with SELECT 1 failed the removed-metadata case; the source was restored in a finally block. The first commit gate caught an invalid acceptance-map tier label (`e2e` instead of the registry's `end-to-end`); it was corrected. CLI integration tests no longer replace console or emit signals on the test process. A separate CLI process is started and stopped in its smoke test.

## Bounded event inspection

The engine previously accumulated every event view into one array. Board now owns a streamed count- and byte-limited page reader; the engine, HTTP client, CLI and MCP share explicit after/limit selectors and the events/next_cursor/has_more response. The page budget includes envelope and separator bytes. A single event too large for a page fails, future cursors fail with HTTP 409, and invalid or ambiguous selectors fail. Client response validation returns typed transport errors. The earlier preview array shape is intentionally replaced and documented.

The first full paging run passed the new cases but exposed two old tests that assumed the array response and old help options. Those assertions now check the page contract and added selectors. The next full offline run passed 492 tests across 46 files in 42.19 seconds with complete measured coverage and 96 product sources, zero gaps. Shared cases exercise HTTP, CLI and MCP paging, future cursors, invalid selectors, byte limits and malformed server responses. The installed probe also follows two-event CLI pages to check contiguous ids. Later changes add an explicit durable final-event wait to avoid basing the fixture's expected history on an in-flight in-memory terminal state.

Task 14.2 remains open: JSON paging is implemented, but live SSE replay and reconnect handoff are not. Paging validates earlier journal entries by scanning them; byte-offset indexing is not implemented. Another identified delivery concern is that in-memory state can change before its pending commit completes; external observation must be reviewed against the durable-acknowledgment requirement. Continue with that boundary and active vendor processes after pushing this increment.

## Durable external snapshots

External HTTP observations previously returned the engine's mutable run object. That object could already say passed or running while its write was pending. RunStore now retains a bounded committed record and returns a detached clone after its queued-write barrier. Replay initializes a separate committed copy. External status, list, submission and control responses use engine snapshots. Event requests capture a committed version and stop replay there, rather than exposing a concurrent unfinished append.

A failed chain stays failed; projection failure can leave a valid authoritative journal entry, which explicit reload preserves. The prior failed-resume fixture incorrectly expected 200; it now requires 500 on resume and subsequent reads while proving the health endpoint remains available. Journal byte-budget fixtures now page their actual writer's committed snapshot instead of a separate engine's stale cache. A replay-cutoff case excludes later records.

## Managed native process recovery in progress

The runtime now hosts each Linux native turn in a detached process group. A persistent candidate SQLite lease prevents overlapping writers, including across homes. Native events use a one-event acknowledgment window and bounded typed frames; answers await group teardown. The host kills its ordinary descendants after daemon disconnection. Installed archives include the host entry. Owner-only configuration controls GC marker workers and the readiness deadline. This is lifecycle management for trusted agents, not confinement against deliberate process-group escape.

New source and installed cases kill a daemon while each SDK's worker and descendant are active. They inspect stopped process identities and an unchanged heartbeat, preserve partial files and native identity on resume, and require attempt one with zero refutations. ## Bounded workspace inventory in progress

Workspace traversal is split from streamed source reading and path validation. It uses opendir rather than retaining whole directory listings. Central schemas now define configurable entry, byte and depth budgets with fixed upper bounds. Directories consume entries before descent, file chunks share a byte budget, and copies validate their manifests before creating targets. The engine propagates frozen limits through snapshots and validates each combined ancestor manifest. A growing source cannot write an excess chunk before its mismatch is detected. These are snapshot limits, not live agent disk quotas.

## Claude tool-specific path repair in progress

Both mandatory callbacks now use one input policy. Read, Write and Edit require file_path; Glob and Grep use path and validate their pattern fields. A conflicting file_path cannot hide a search path. Glob patterns and Grep glob filters must be relative, with no parent marker, home expansion, backslashes, null bytes or extended parentheses syntax. Brace alternatives must remain within one path component and cannot nest. Missing, null or malformed input fails closed. Existing outside-symlink parent checks remain active. This is still a file-tool policy with filesystem race limits, not a complete OS sandbox or shell permission implementation.

Pinned Claude SDK 0.3.319 sdk-tools.d.ts declares GlobInput.pattern plus optional path, and GrepInput.pattern, optional path and optional glob. ## Native Codex tool exposure investigation

Read-only research used Codex CLI 0.167.0 against a synthetic Responses endpoint inside the existing offline Podman image, without credentials, a host home or a network interface. Temporary script /tmp/titan-codex-tool-probe.ts is a research probe outside the default test suite. The isolated CODEX_HOME directory had to be created explicitly; the first probe exited before any model request because it was missing. The default gpt-6.1-sol metadata omitted tools from the captured request even when shell_tool was enabled. An explicit gpt-5.3-codex probe used fallback metadata and exposed exec_command, write_stdin and other control tools with the feature enabled. Therefore a single tool-free request cannot establish a policy.

## Durable scoped request owner in progress

The requests package now owns bounded typed operation requests through a journal port. Constants define request kinds, default approval levels, versions and ceilings; types own strict schemas and the journal contract. A request binds its run, origin, scope, exact arguments, environment names, content hashes and expiry. The ledger serializes answers per run, rejects stale or repeated answers, checks authenticated host authority, and refuses denied, expired or changed-scope approval use. Replay rejects duplicate openings, rewritten scope, missing openings, invalid authority and invalid decision time. The run allowance counts answered requests and the mutation queue is capped. Existing journal event data carries these versioned records without changing stored run shape or rewriting history.

The host must share one ledger and run writer and must authenticate actors before calling it. No credential is accepted through the actor object itself. This package alone does not provide person tokens, CLI/HTTP/MCP approval routes, configuration overrides, paused agent requests, script execution or action reconciliation. Existing scripts remain blocked. Broad tasks 9.1, 16.1, 16.2, 16.3 and 22.10 remain open; the existing HTTP and script acceptance scenarios remain pending. No human-presence or product-readiness claim follows from this increment.

## Persistent person authentication in progress

The server now creates and retains a private person token under the home lease. Existing token files require a regular current-user-owned file, mode 600, one link and exactly 64 random hexadecimal characters. The agent address never includes this token; the agent token rotates on restart. Authenticated clients receive main or human authority internally. Web-page Origin headers receive 403 before token checks. Unsafe files stop startup without replacement; repairing a fixture permits a fresh server. Same-user agents may read the token, so this does not prove human presence or per-client isolation. Approval routes, CLI commands and configuration remain separate unfinished work.

## Explicit CLI request decisions in progress

The CLI now has a separate injected request-client port, nested single-request inspection and explicit agent answer versus person approve/deny handlers. Every decision requires exact run/request ids, observed version, answering client and reason. Person transport reads an existing validated token and never creates one; agent permission failures do not select it automatically. The typed client admits bounded strict JSON before parsing and binds response identity, scope, answer/version/reason and actor. ## Request admission across recovery transitions

Request-origin increment 0b0fb6c74a7a4dae23e12f196b790b0e670de39b committed and pushed normally. Native waiting, continuation and complete product acceptance remain open.

Read-only SDK research used the OpenAI Docs skill and the installed pinned SDK declarations. The official [Codex SDK page](https://developers.openai.com/codex/sdk) documents continuing or resuming a retained thread, and the [MCP page](https://developers.openai.com/codex/mcp) documents stdio and HTTP server configuration. Neither establishes that aborting a running SDK stream preserves a completed request tool result. Claude's pinned declarations expose hooks and interrupt, but a declaration alone does not prove a lossless boundary. Native request waiting needs its own durable checkpoint and offline process counterexamples before any live acceptance. No Titan skill was loaded and no vendor call was made.

A prerequisite counterexample found that a failed phase could still open a new bound request while its recovery commit was held and its node remained running. The controlled table initially failed all four cases because openings succeeded. Live admission now requires a clear operational diagnostic and no scheduled retry, and live append repeats that check. New execution-start commits clear the old diagnostic; otherwise a correctly resumed session would also be refused. Historical replay deliberately retains the earlier lineage rule rather than declaring previously admitted records corrupt.

The expanded table holds worker and checker recovery commits, holds healthy snapshots across failure, verifies same-session new execution admission with unchanged quality attempt and empty evidence, and reloads historical opening states containing old diagnostics and retry times. ## Opened snapshot descriptor validation

The shared workspace source reader previously opened with no-follow protection but did not verify the descriptor type. A null device could therefore become an empty snapshot, while a zero device reached the downstream byte budget instead of being refused as a special source. A pipe replacement could wait for a writer during opening. The draft opens in nonblocking mode with existing no-follow protection, checks the opened descriptor with stat before streaming, and reports unsupported_file using the source path. Its existing executable checks and finally-owned close remain in place. Hashing and copying use this same reader.

Linux documents that nonblocking read opening avoids waiting for a pipe writer and has no effect on regular-file reads. The descriptor check is still required because nonblocking mode alone does not classify a source. The regression suite directly covers regular empty and populated files, null and zero devices, and a directory. It checks descriptor cleanup under the source parent and the temporary directory. It does not create a FIFO or simulate a path replacement race. Sources are the [open manual](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man2/open.2.html) and [FIFO manual](https://man7.org/linux/man-pages/man7/fifo.7.html).

Successful typechecking preceded runtime tests. The first focused selection passed 25 cases across three files in 570 milliseconds. Two type-correct faults were caught and restored. Removing regular-file refusal failed three cases, and retaining the handle failed all five new cases. Restored typechecking passed, then the same 25 cases passed in 563 milliseconds. Both focused commands exited one for 212 omitted whole-product coverage gaps. Evidence is /tmp/titan-source-descriptor-first.log, /tmp/titan-source-descriptor-mutants.json, /tmp/titan-source-descriptor-restored-types.log and /tmp/titan-source-descriptor-restored.log. The reference structure checker found zero findings across all five changed source files. Biome, flat cases and specification checks passed. ## Scoped configuration writer increment

Task 29.5 is in progress on codex/config-file-writes. The draft adds config init, validate, schema and set subcommands with explicit user or project scope. Existing effective inspection remains compatible. Shared file schemas and error codes live in their owning packages. The writer reuses configuration layer validation, bounded file reads and the durable atomic writer. Its version is the exact previous file byte hash, including formatting changes. A private SQLite lease serializes cooperating writers. Successful writes use JSON in the YAML file and replace comments and formatting. Metadata output omits settings values.

The first race selection caught four failures. SQLite's 250 millisecond busy wait blocked the main event loop and prevented the first writer from releasing its lease. The corrected writer disables that busy wait and yields between ten bounded 25 millisecond retries. The unchanged one-winner and one-version-conflict assertions passed. Later selected cases also cover held and corrupt leases, private modes, owner policy, unsafe keys and CLI subprocesses. The latest selection passed 74 tests across three files in 5.88 seconds.
